## PMID 29436835

Redman JS (2018) Pharmacokinetic and Chemical Synthesis Optimization of a Potent d-Peptide HIV Entry Inhibitor Suitable for Extended-Release Delivery. *Mol Pharm*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.molpharmaceut.7b01004

- Contributes **16 records** (15 distinct, the rest are exact repeats), 1 unique sequences; 16 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 16 of 16 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC5893306).
- Reported half-life: 0.6-7.2 h (median 1.8 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV|SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 8 agents, 7 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_reported|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS/MS and UHPLC-Q-TOF MS on acetonitrile-precipitated plasma, quantified against an 8-point calibration curve with a Chol-PEG12-PIE12-trimer internal standard.
- Body-quoted half-lives: PIE12-trimer: 0.6 h (IV, range 0.5-0.8); 0.8 h (SC, range 0.7-0.9) [rat plasma] ;; Palm-PIE12-trimer: 1.8 h (IV, range 1.4-1.9); 2.2 h (SC, range 1.6-3.3) [rat plasma] ;; C16-PIE12-trimer: 0.9 h (IV, range 0.9-1.0); 1.2 h (SC, range 1.1-1.6) [rat plasma] ;; C18-PIE12-trimer: 1.1 h (IV, range 1.0-1.1); 1.4 h (SC, range 1.2-1.4) [rat plasma] ;; Chol-PIE12-trimer: Table 2: 1.8 h IV, 2.7 h SC; repeat study Table 3: 1.6 h IV, 3.9 h SC [rat plasma] ;; Chol-PEG5k-PIE12-trimer: 5.6 h (IV, range 4.5-5.7); 7.2 h (SC, range 7.2-7.9) [rat plasma] ;; CPT31: rat: 3.3 h IV, 5.4 h SC; cynomolgus monkey: 7.4 h IV, 18.8 h SC [rat plasma (monkey plasma for the NHP values)].
- Caveats: All rat half-lives are stated as median (range) of elimination half-life, not mean +/- SD; the table footnote defines T1/2=elimination half-life, so pk_phase is unambiguous. Chol-PIE12-trimer was run twice under different CROs (Navigen Table 2, Calvert Table 3) and PEPlife2 carries all four of its values as separate records, which is correct. The PEPlife2 rows for this PMID reproduce Tables 2 and 3 exactly; no correction needed. PEG40-PIE12-trimer's ~15-fold figure is for PIE12-monomer, not the trimer, and no absolute half-life is given, so it is left unreported.

- Caveats: PEPlife2 carries 11 rows for this PMID - five values for 'Peptides 1' (10.9, 12.2, 10.7, 18.6, 12.9 h) and six for 'Peptides 2' (8.5, 8.0, 8.6, 7.7, 7.9, 8.7 h). Those per-animal or per-route values are not in the article body: the body reports only 'approximately 10 h' for 1 and '8.4 h' for 2, and states the remaining PK parameters are in Tables S1-S4, which are supplementary and absent from the PMC BioC deposit. The transcribed values could not be verified against the body and are left uncorrected; note the body's 8.4 h for peptide 2 is close to the mean of PEPlife2's six values for it, which is consistent but not a verification. Body does not print the amino-acid sequence for either peptide; PEPlife2's sequences were not confirmable from the text.

## PMID 36588717

He L (2022) Pharmacokinetics, distribution, metabolism, and excretion of body-protective compound 157, a potential drug for treating various wounds, in rats and dogs. *Front Pharmacol*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.3389/fphar.2022.1026182

- Contributes **10 records**, 1 unique sequences; 10 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 10 of 10 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC9794587).
- Reported half-life: 0.131-102 h (median 0.297 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IM|IM, Multiple doses|IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 7 agents, 7 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: im|iv.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS/MS with solid-phase extraction from 50 μl plasma, dextromethorphan internal standard, ESI on a tandem quadrupole; linear range 4.00-4,000 ng/ml. Total radioactivity by liquid scintillation counting..
- Body-quoted half-lives: BPC157 (intravenous, rat): 15.2 min [rat plasma] ;; BPC157 (intramuscular 20 μg/kg, rat): 7.87 min [rat plasma] ;; BPC157 (intramuscular 100 μg/kg, rat): 17.1 min [rat plasma] ;; BPC157 (intramuscular 500 μg/kg, rat): 29.7 min [rat plasma] ;; BPC157 (repeat intramuscular 100 μg/kg x 7 days, rat): 18.5 min [rat plasma] ;; [3H]BPC157 (total radioactivity, JVC rat): 102 ± 32 h [rat plasma, total radioactivity (not parent drug)] ;; BPC157 (beagle dog): 5.27 ± 2.25 min (IV); 20.0, 25.5, 29.3 min (IM 6, 30, 150 μg/kg); 19.6 ± 3.72 min (repeat IM) [dog plasma].
- Caveats: All six distinct PEPlife2 half-lives for this PMID were verified against the body: 15.2 min (Table 1, IV), 7.87/17.1/29.7 min (Table 2, IM 20/100/500 μg/kg), 18.5 min (Table 3, repeat IM), and 102 ± 32 h (Table 9). PEPlife2's ten rows are these six values with four duplicated under a second Test Sample spelling ('SD rats plasma' vs 'Rats plasma'). IMPORTANT: the 102 h value is the half-life of TOTAL RADIOACTIVITY after [3H]BPC157, not of the parent peptide - the paper states the prototype drug was undetectable 4 h after dosing and its elimination half-life is under 30 min. Treating 102 h as a BPC157 plasma half-life would overstate it by ~200-fold. The body does not print the amino-acid sequence.

## PMID 35703463

Li Y (2022) Evaluation of Physicochemical Properties, Pharmacokinetics, Biodistribution, Toxicity, and Contrast-Enhanced Cancer MRI of a Cancer-Targeting Contrast Agent, MT218. *Invest Radiol*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1097/RLI.0000000000000881

- Contributes **9 records** (7 distinct, the rest are exact repeats), 1 unique sequences; 9 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 9 of 9 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC9444296).
- Reported half-life: 0.22-0.65 h (median 0.31 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo|in vitro; 5 agents, 5 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: plasma.
- Bioanalytical method: Validated ICP-MS (Agilent 7800) quantitation of Gd in plasma with terbium internal standard; non-compartmental analysis in WinNonlin 8.0.
- Body-quoted half-lives: MT218 (0.04 mmol/kg, rat): 0.30 h (male); 0.28 h (female) [rat plasma (Gd(III) measured by ICP-MS)] ;; MT218 (0.1 mmol/kg, rat): 0.31 h (male); 0.32 h (female) [rat plasma (Gd(III) measured by ICP-MS)] ;; MT218 (0.2 mmol/kg, rat): 0.30 h (male); 0.32 h (female) [rat plasma (Gd(III) measured by ICP-MS)] ;; MT218 (beagle dog): 0.76/0.74 h (0.04); 0.70/0.67 h (0.1); 0.69/0.63 h (0.2), male/female [dog plasma (Gd(III) by ICP-MS)] ;; gadoteridol (ProHance): approximately 0.39 h (rat); 0.78 h (dog) [rat and dog plasma].
- Caveats: SERIOUS TRANSCRIPTION ERROR IN PEPlife2. Table 3's 'Sex' column has three levels - male, female, and P - where P is the significance of the male-vs-female comparison, per the footnote 'P value: independent t test, female vs male'. PEPlife2 read that statistics row as a third experimental group and transcribed the p-values 0.57, 0.65 and 0.22 as elimination half-lives in hours (PEPlife2 IDs 4577, 4580, 4583, all labelled '(P) SD rats plasma'). They are not half-lives and have been excluded via CURATED_NOT_A_HALF_LIFE in subset_in_vivo_half_life.py. The four genuine values PEPlife2 does carry (0.30, 0.28, 0.31, 0.32 h) are correct; it omits the 0.2 mmol/kg pair (0.30, 0.32 h), which duplicate values already present. Note also that the half-life here is of Gd(III), measured by ICP-MS, not of the intact peptide - the paper separately reports that the ZD2 peptide itself degrades in rat plasma with a t1/2 of 1.63 h ex vivo. The body does not print the peptide sequence.

## PMID 28714475

Zorzi A (2017) Acylated heptapeptide binds albumin with high affinity and application as tag furnishes long-acting peptides. *Nat Commun*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1038/ncomms16092

- Contributes **7 records**, 4 unique sequences; 7 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 7 of 7 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC5520048).
- Reported half-life: 0.3-12.3 h (median 1.5 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo|in vitro; 4 agents, 4 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: plasma.
- Bioanalytical method: RP-HPLC (Agilent 1260) with fluorescence detection (ex 445 nm, em 535 nm) on ethanol-precipitated plasma, quantified against standard curves in plasma; two-compartment PK model.
- Body-quoted half-lives: tag-PK128: t1/2 alpha 0.9+/-0.2 h; t1/2 beta 2.9+/-0.7 h [rat plasma] ;; tag-UK18: t1/2 alpha 1.0+/-0.1 h; t1/2 beta 7.4+/-0.2 h [rat plasma] ;; UK18 (non-tagged control): t1/2 beta 0.30+/-0.02 h (= 18+/-1 min); t1/2 alpha not determined [rat plasma] ;; tag alone: t1/2 alpha 1.5+/-0.4 h; t1/2 beta 12.3+/-0.4 h [rat plasma].
- Caveats: All seven PEPlife2 values for this PMID were verified against Table 2 and agree exactly: tag-PK128 0.9/2.9, tag-UK18 1.0/7.4, UK18 0.30 (beta only, alpha n.d.), tag 1.5/12.3 h. This is a two-compartment study, so alpha and beta are separate records and must not be pooled - the spread between them within one animal is up to 8-fold. UK18's 0.30 h agrees with the body's independent statement of 18+/-1 min. The rabbit FXIIa-inhibitor arm (5.2+/-0.4 h tagged vs 13+/-2 min untagged) is a different species and is not part of the rat cohort. Note the authors state the tag binds rat albumin five- to sevenfold more weakly than human albumin, so these rat half-lives understate the expected human values.

- Reported half-life: 18.3-51 h (median 28.8 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 1 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.

- Bioanalytical method: Indirect competitive ELISA against an anti-LMRAP monoclonal antibody.
- Body-quoted half-lives: LMRAP: 33.332 +/- 11.189 h (mean of six animals); per-animal t1/2beta 45.237, 29.187, 50.979, 18.256, 27.879, 28.452 h [rat serum].
- Caveats: All six PEPlife2 values verified against Table 2, which reports t1/2beta PER ANIMAL for six rats: 45.237, 29.187, 50.979, 18.256, 27.879, 28.452 h (mean 33.332 +/- 11.189 h). PEPlife2 transcribed each animal as its own record, so this single n=6 experiment contributes six rows while most studies contribute one row per reported mean - an unweighting issue inherent to PEPlife2, not a transcription error. One row carries the typo 'Eliminatoin Half Life' (PEPlife2 ID 5209), which left it classified 'unlabelled' while its five table-mates were 'terminal / beta'; the spelling has been added to the phase regex in subset_in_vivo_half_life.py. Note the reported alpha/Ke column is zero for three of the six animals and the paper states a ONE-compartment model was fitted, so t1/2beta here is the single elimination half-life, not the second phase of a two-phase fit.

- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS/MS of plasma (assay details not given in the body).
- Body-quoted half-lives: CEND-1 (rat, male, 1 mg/kg): 0.805 h [rat plasma] ;; CEND-1 (rat, female, 1 mg/kg): 0.248 h [rat plasma] ;; CEND-1 (rat, male, 5 mg/kg): 0.46 h [rat plasma] ;; CEND-1 (rat, female, 5 mg/kg): 0.437 h [rat plasma] ;; CEND-1 (rat, male, 75 mg/kg): 0.341 h [rat plasma] ;; CEND-1 (rat, female, 75 mg/kg): 0.391 h [rat plasma] ;; CEND-1 (mouse): 0.306, 0.344, 0.547 h [mouse plasma].
- Caveats: All six PEPlife2 values verified exactly against Table 1's rat block: 0.805 (M, 1 mg/kg), 0.248 (F, 1), 0.460 (M, 5), 0.437 (F, 5), 0.341 (M, 75), 0.391 (F, 75) h. Two cautions. (1) The body's Methods say the rat doses were '1, 5 and 50 mg/kg' but Table 1 reports the top rat dose as 75 mg/kg; the discrepancy is the paper's and the table value is used here. (2) Table 1's footnote states each rat was sampled at ONE time point only, so these half-lives are fitted across animals rather than within an animal - the 3.2-fold spread between the male and female 1 mg/kg values (0.805 vs 0.248 h) should be read with that in mind, not as a sex effect. The body's summary '30 min in rats' is the rough average across all six.

- Curated extraction: study types in vivo; 6 agents, 6 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: sc.
- Bioanalytical method: Two ELISAs on the same plasma samples, one for mature AM (mAM) and one for total AM (tAM); PK parameters computed in WinNonlin.
- Body-quoted half-lives: IgG1 S-AM (6-52), mature AM readout: 2.110 days [rat plasma, mature AM (mAM) ELISA] ;; IgG4 S-AM (6-52), mature AM readout: 2.370 days [rat plasma, mature AM (mAM) ELISA] ;; IgG1 S-AM (6-52), total AM readout: 2.885 days [rat plasma, total AM (tAM) ELISA] ;; IgG4 S-AM (6-52), total AM readout: 3.235 days [rat plasma, total AM (tAM) ELISA] ;; native AM, mature AM readout: 32.3 min [rat plasma, mature AM (mAM) ELISA] ;; native AM, total AM readout: 27.5 min [rat plasma, total AM (tAM) ELISA].
- Caveats: All six PEPlife2 values verified against Table 1 and the Results text (PEPlife2's 3.23 d is the text's rounding of Table 1's 3.235 d). Two cautions for downstream use. (1) mAM and tAM are two ELISA READOUTS of the SAME dosed animals, not two experiments - IgG1 contributes both 2.11 d and 2.885 d, and treating them as independent records double-counts each construct. (2) The Fc-AM arms are n = 2 per group, and the whole study used 8 rats; these half-lives carry very little precision and no dispersion is reported. Native AM was dosed at 50 nmol/kg while the Fc fusions were dosed at 30 nmol/kg, so the comparison is not dose-matched. Sequences are PEPlife2's; the body does not print them.

## PMID 29517911

Teufel DP (2018) Stable and Long-Lasting, Novel Bicyclic Peptide Plasma Kallikrein Inhibitors for the Treatment of Diabetic Macular Edema. *J Med Chem*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.jmedchem.7b01625

- Contributes **6 records**, 3 unique sequences; 5 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: ex vivo (fluid stability)|in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vitro|in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 3 of 6 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction**.
- Reported half-life: 0.8-24 h (median 2.8 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV|N.A..
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo; 2 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: other|plasma.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS/MS per PEPlife2's Assay field; the PDF text does not yield a recoverable bioanalytical description.
- Body-quoted half-lives: peptide 1b: ~78 min [rat plasma] ;; peptide 2d: ~48 min [rat plasma].
- Caveats: Read from the publisher PDF; the renderer reported this file as having no separable abstract, so the abstract sits at the top of the body and was identified and avoided. Both PEPlife2 values verified against the Results text: ~78 min for 1b and ~48 min for 2d, both approximate. The body gives no strain, sex or group size; the 5 mg/kg IV dose comes from PEPlife2's own Dose Route and Concentration fields rather than from recoverable body text. SEQUENCE ENCODING: both strings carry non-natural residues written as parenthesised or lowercase codes - (Sar)3 (three sarcosines) and lowercase rr (two D-arginines) in 1b, and hArg (homoarginine) in 2d. The hyphen-delimited codes are now collapsed to a single X each by clean_sequence, but '(Sar)3' encodes a repeat count that no single-character substitution can express, and the lowercase D-residue markers are lost to upper-casing. This paper also reports a ~40 h half-life in rabbit EYES after intravitreal dosing, which is a different species and compartment and is not part of this cohort.

## PMID 28605180

Schneider EL (2017) A Hydrogel-Microsphere Drug Delivery System That Supports Once-Monthly Administration of a GLP-1 Receptor Agonist. *ACS Chem Biol*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1021/acschembio.7b00218

- Contributes **6 records**, 2 unique sequences; 6 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 6 of 6 records.
- Assay matrices: serum.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction**.
- Reported half-life: 0.283-880 h (median 0.817 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV|SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 6 agents, 6 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS/MS of serum (assay detail not recoverable from the PDF text).
- Body-quoted half-lives: MS-[Gln28]exenatide microspheres (MeSO2- modulator): 310 hr [rat serum] ;; MS-[Gln28]exenatide hydrogel microspheres (-CN modulator): 880 hr [rat serum] ;; exenatide (IV): 18 min [rat serum] ;; exenatide (SC, absorption phase): 50 min [rat serum] ;; [Gln28]exenatide (IV): 17 min [rat serum] ;; [Gln28]exenatide (SC, absorption phase): 48 min [rat serum].
- Caveats: Read from the publisher PDF, not a PMC deposit. Four of six PEPlife2 values verified directly: 310 hr (Fig. 5A), 880 hr (Fig. 5B), 18 min and 17 min. TWO ARE ABSORPTION HALF-LIVES, NOT ELIMINATION. The body states the SC data follow a flip-flop mechanism with 'rate-determining absorption having a t1/2 of 50 min, followed by an elimination phase with t1/2 of 18 min'. PEPlife2 records those 50 min and 48 min SC values as plain half-lives beside the 18 min and 17 min IV values for the same two peptides, so each peptide appears twice with a 2.8-fold spread that is a phase difference, not a molecular one. PEPlife2 IDs 5998 and 6000 are dropped via CURATED_ABSORPTION, matching how the pipeline already treats every labelled absorption value. Note also the body reports exenatide and [Gln28]exenatide as pharmacokinetically INDISTINGUISHABLE in rodents, so the 18-vs-17 and 50-vs-48 min pairs should not be read as a sequence effect; the second of each pair comes from supplementary Table S3.

## PMID 31818212

Sun J (2019) Human Relaxin-2 Fusion Protein Treatment Prevents and Reverses Isoproterenol-Induced Hypertrophy and Fibrosis in Mouse Heart. *J Am Heart Assoc*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1161/JAHA.119.013465

- Contributes **5 records**, 1 unique sequences; 5 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 5 of 5 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC6951077).
- Reported half-life: 86.2-169 h (median 134 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV|SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 6 agents, 6 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: infusion_pump|iv|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: ELISA with anti-Fc monoclonal antibody TM446 (AstraZeneca) as capture and the HRP-conjugated polyclonal from the R&D Systems Relaxin-2 detection kit as detection.
- Body-quoted half-lives: RELAX10 (rat, IV): 3.59 days [rat plasma] ;; RELAX10 (rat, SC): 3.75 days [rat plasma] ;; RELAX10 (mouse, IV): 5.58 days [mouse plasma] ;; RELAX10 (mouse, SC): 7.06 days [mouse plasma] ;; RELAX10 (mouse, SC dose escalation): 4.59 to 7.06 days (range across three dose levels) [mouse plasma] ;; relaxin-2 peptide (serelaxin): 1.1 to 2 minutes in rodents (cited from reference 25, not measured here); 240 minutes in humans (reference 24) [not applicable].
- Caveats: SPECIES MISLABELLING IN PEPlife2. All five records for this PMID are filed as 'Wistar rats plasma', but the body assigns only two of them to rats. The rat study is 4 mg/kg IV and SC, giving 3.59 and 3.75 days (PEPlife2 IDs 5299, 5300). The 5.58 d and 7.06 d values are the MOUSE study at 6 mg/kg (IDs 5301, 5302), and '4.59 - 7.06 days' is the range across the MOUSE subcutaneous dose escalation at 1, 6 and 30 mg/kg (ID 5303). IDs 5301 and 5302 have been reassigned to mouse via CURATED_SPECIES, and 5303 has been dropped via CURATED_DUPLICATES because it is a range whose upper bound is already recorded as 5302 and whose midpoint is a number the paper never states. Left uncorrected this study would have contributed three mouse half-lives to the rat cohort, all at the long end of the distribution. The discussion's summary - 'a terminal half-life of 7 and 3.75 days in mice and rats, respectively, after subcutaneous administration' - confirms the assignment independently.

## PMID 35414877

Bhushan B (2022) An integrated platform approach enables discovery of potent, selective and ligand-competitive cyclic peptides targeting the GIP receptor. *Chem Sci*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1039/d1sc06844j

- Contributes **5 records**, 4 unique sequences; 5 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 5 of 5 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC8926291).
- Reported half-life: 0.0395-10.7 h (median 4.17 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo|in vitro; 5 agents, 5 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: plasma.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS of plasma (detail not given in the body).
- Body-quoted half-lives: B_3.1: 9.8 h [rat plasma] ;; B_1275.1: 10.7 h [rat plasma] ;; B_1275.4: 3.5 min [rat plasma] ;; B_1275.5: 1.8 min [rat plasma] ;; B_1275.6: 4.2 h [rat plasma].
- Caveats: Three of five PEPlife2 values agree with the body: 585 min = 9.75 h vs the body's 9.8 h for B_3.1; 643 min = 10.72 h vs 10.7 h for B_1275.1; 250 min = 4.17 h vs 4.2 h for B_1275.6. The other two do NOT match the body: PEPlife2 gives 3.767 min for B_1275.4 where the body says 3.5 min, and 2.367 min for B_1275.5 where the body says 1.8 min (a 31% difference). The precise values are presumably in Table S2 of the ESI, which is not in the PMC deposit, so the disagreement cannot be resolved from the body and neither value is auto-applied. Both are sub-5-minute half-lives, so the absolute discrepancy is small. The body reports no dose, no strain and no sex for the rat study; n refers to three independent experiments, not three animals. For scale the authors note semaglutide has a rat t1/2 of 7 h, below B_3.1 and B_1275.1 here.

## PMID 33665501

Jiang Z (2021) Poly(l-glutamic acid)-cisplatin nanoformulations with detachable PEGylation for prolonged circulation half-life and enhanced cell internalization. *Bioact Mater*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.bioactmat.2021.01.034

- Contributes **5 records**, 2 unique sequences; 4 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 5 of 5 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC7895728).
- Reported half-life: 1.9-8.8 h (median 7.85 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 5 agents, 5 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Bioanalytical method: ICP-MS (PerkinElmer NexION) of nitric-acid-digested plasma for platinum content; parameters computed in PKSolver.
- Body-quoted half-lives: PLG-Pt: 1.9 h [rat plasma - PLATINUM content by ICP-MS, not peptide or polymer concentration] ;; PEG-PLG-Pt: 8.8 h [rat plasma - PLATINUM content by ICP-MS, not peptide or polymer concentration] ;; PEG-pHe-PLG-Pt: 7.9 h [rat plasma - PLATINUM content by ICP-MS, not peptide or polymer concentration] ;; PEG-MMP-PLG-Pt: 7.8 h [rat plasma - PLATINUM content by ICP-MS, not peptide or polymer concentration] ;; free cisplatin (CDDP): 0.4 h [rat plasma, platinum by ICP-MS].
- Caveats: EXCLUDED FROM THE TRAINING SET on two independent grounds, both confirmed here. (1) The measured analyte is PLATINUM by ICP-MS, i.e. cisplatin, not a peptide - the four values 1.9, 8.8, 7.9 and 7.8 h are the drug's plasma half-life and the paper attributes their differences to the PEG shell shielding the nanoparticle from clearance, not to any peptide. (2) PEPlife2's Sequence field for these records is not a sequence but the polymer's NAME, 'Poly(L-glutamic acid)' (and 'PLGLAG-Poly(L-glutamic acid)' for the MMP-cleavable variant), which the training-set sequence cleaner reduces to the residue string POLYLGLUTAMICACID. PEPlife2 IDs 4968, 4969, 4970 and 4972 are dropped via CURATED_NOT_A_HALF_LIFE. A fifth record, PEG-pHe-PLG-Pt at pH 6.5, carries the literal value 'N.A.' and is removed earlier by the requirement for a numeric half-life. Only the PLGLAG hexapeptide in PEG-MMP-PLG-Pt is an actual peptide, and it is an MMP-2-cleavable linker within the polymer, not the dosed agent.

- Curated extraction: study types in vivo; 4 agents, 4 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: Radioimmunoassay for CNP immunoreactivity using antiserum against the 17-membered internal disulfide ring of CNP and [125I]-[Tyr0]-CNP(1-22) tracer.
- Body-quoted half-lives: CNP(1-22) (IV): 1.42 ± 0.45 min [rat plasma, CNP immunoreactivity] ;; ASB20123 (IV): 31.3 ± 4.8 min [rat plasma, CNP immunoreactivity] ;; CNP(1-22) (SC): 10.0 ± 5.0 min [rat plasma, CNP immunoreactivity] ;; ASB20123 (SC): 32.1 ± 8.0 min [rat plasma, CNP immunoreactivity].
- Caveats: All four PEPlife2 values verified against the IV and SC parameter tables: CNP(1-22) 1.42 ± 0.45 min IV and 10.0 ± 5.0 min SC; ASB20123 31.3 ± 4.8 min IV and 32.1 ± 8.0 min SC. PEPlife2 records the last as a bare '32.1', dropping the ±8.0. TWO CORRECTIONS TO PEPlife2 METADATA. (1) The assay is recorded as 'ELISA' but the body says RADIOIMMUNOASSAY, with an antiserum against the CNP disulfide ring and a [125I]-[Tyr0]-CNP(1-22) tracer - an immunoreactivity readout that does not distinguish intact peptide from ring-containing fragments. (2) PEPlife2 records no route, so the IV and SC values for each peptide are indistinguishable in the database; for CNP(1-22) the SC value is 7-fold the IV value, a flip-flop absorption effect rather than a change in clearance. The rats were anaesthetised (pentobarbital, later Inactin) for the whole PK study, which the paper notes but does not control for.

## PMID 38486997

Zhao Q (2024) Design and discovery of a highly potent ultralong-acting GLP-1 and glucagon co-agonist for attenuating renal fibrosis. *Acta Pharm Sin B*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.apsb.2023.11.020

- Contributes **4 records**, 2 unique sequences; 4 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 4 of 4 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC10935026).
- Reported half-life: 7.95-20.5 h (median 12.4 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV|SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 5 agents, 5 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: In-house LC-MS/MS method; non-compartmental analysis in WinNonlin 6.4.
- Body-quoted half-lives: 1907-B (IV): ~16.8 h [rat plasma] ;; semaglutide (IV): ~8 h [rat plasma] ;; 1907-B (SC): ~20.5 h [rat plasma] ;; semaglutide (SC): ~7.95 h [rat plasma] ;; 1907-B (cynomolgus monkey, SC): 84 h [monkey plasma].
- Caveats: All four PEPlife2 values verified exactly against the Results text: IV 1907-B ~16.8 h and semaglutide ~8 h; SC 1907-B ~20.5 h and semaglutide ~7.95 h. The paper reports them as approximate ('~'), which PEPlife2 preserves. SEQUENCE ENCODING ISSUE, now fixed upstream: both sequences carry an Aib (alpha-aminoisobutyric acid) residue written as a hyphen-delimited three-letter code - 'HsQGTFTSDKSKYLE-Aib-KAAQDFVEWLKAGGPSSGAPPPS' and 'H-Aib-EGTFTSDVSSYLEGQAAKEFIAWLVRGRG'. The training-set sequence cleaner previously upper-cased and stripped punctuation, turning that ONE residue into THREE standard ones (A, I, B); clean_sequence in build_halflife_training_set.py now collapses hyphen-delimited non-natural codes to a single X. Aib appears in 52 records across 10 studies and Sar in 46 across 13, so this affected far more than this paper. Note also the semaglutide sequence here ends ...LVRGRG rather than the canonical ...LVRGRG with the C18 diacid protractor, which PEPlife2 does not encode. PEPlife2 records no route, so the IV and SC pairs are indistinguishable in the database.

## PMID 36997003

Novikoff A (2023) The molecular pharmacology of glucagon agonists in diabetes and obesity. *Peptides*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.peptides.2023.171003

- Contributes **4 records**, 2 unique sequences; 4 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 4 of 4 records.
- Assay matrices: serum.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC10265134).
- Reported half-life: 40.9-66.4 h (median 53.7 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV|SC.
- Curated extraction: study types n/a; 1 agents, 0 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Bioanalytical method: not reported in body.
- Caveats: THIS IS A REVIEW ARTICLE, not a primary study - PubMed publication types are 'Journal Article | Research Support, Non-U.S. Gov't | Review', and the paper presents no experiment of its own. PEPlife2 nonetheless records four half-life values against it (66.4 ± 33.0 and 40.9 ± 17.7 h, each duplicated once for the peptide-only and the peptide-plus-Fc sequence, filed as 'Rats serum'). NEITHER VALUE APPEARS IN THE BODY. The only half-life this review states for HM15136 is 'an enhanced 36 hr half-life', given without species, dose, route or citationable detail in the body text; 66.4 and 40.9 h are presumably from the primary HM15136 publication, which this review cites. Per the body-only rule these are recorded as 'not reported in body'. The values have NOT been excluded from the dataset, because they are plausibly real primary measurements; instead a source_is_review flag has been added to build_study_dossier.py and now propagates to in_vivo_half_life.csv, marking these and the 40 other records that come from the cohort's four review articles so they can be weighted or removed deliberately. Note the two PEPlife2 sequences differ (peptide alone vs peptide + IgG4 Fc) while carrying the SAME two half-lives, so this one study contributes two contradictory sequence-to-label mappings.

## PMID 33918853

Yin S (2021) Engineered Human Heavy-Chain Ferritin with Half-Life Extension and Tumor Targeting by PAS and RGDK Peptide Functionalization. *Pharmaceutics*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.3390/pharmaceutics13040521

- Contributes **4 records**, 4 unique sequences; 4 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 4 of 4 records.
- Assay matrices: serum.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC8070472).
- Reported half-life: 3.07-18.9 h (median 16.3 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 5 agents, 5 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Bioanalytical method: Doxorubicin fluorescence in serum (ex 480 nm, em 580 nm) on a SpectraMax i3x plate reader against a DOX-in-rat-serum standard curve; single-compartment fit in Drug Analysis System 2.0.
- Body-quoted half-lives: HFn/DOX: 3.07 ± 0.06 h [rat serum - DOXORUBICIN fluorescence, not protein concentration] ;; HFn-PAS/DOX: 14.96 ± 0.29 h [rat serum - DOXORUBICIN fluorescence, not protein concentration] ;; HFn-GFLG-PAS-RGDK/DOX: 17.61 ± 0.39 h [rat serum - DOXORUBICIN fluorescence, not protein concentration] ;; HFn-PLGLAG-PAS-RGDK/DOX: 18.93 ± 0.61 h [rat serum - DOXORUBICIN fluorescence, not protein concentration] ;; free doxorubicin: 0.42 ± 0.03 h [rat serum].
- Caveats: All four PEPlife2 values verified exactly against Table 3: 3.07 ± 0.06, 14.96 ± 0.29, 17.61 ± 0.39 and 18.93 ± 0.61 h. THE MEASURED ANALYTE IS DOXORUBICIN, by its own fluorescence, not the ferritin protein - no protein assay is performed. These records are nonetheless retained, unlike the fasudil records of 31194563, because here the protein IS the carrier rather than a surface ligand, all four rows share the same dose and route, and the label tracks the protein engineering monotonically (3.07 h for bare HFn, 14.96 h once PAS is fused, 17.61 and 18.93 h with RGDK added). Free DOX clears with a 0.42 h half-life, so the signal is dominated by the carrier. The caveat that remains is that these are DOX-release-and-clearance half-lives, an upper-bounded proxy for the protein's own circulation time, not a direct measurement of it. Sequences are PEPlife2's concatenations including hyphen-delimited linkers; the hyphens are stripped by the training-set sequence cleaner.

## PMID 31194563

Keshavarz A (2019) CAR, a Homing Peptide, Prolongs Pulmonary Preferential Vasodilation by Increasing Pulmonary Retention and Reducing Systemic Absorption of Liposomal Fasudil. *Mol Pharm*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.molpharmaceut.9b00208

- Contributes **4 records**, 1 unique sequences; 4 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 4 of 4 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC7035787).
- Reported half-life: 0.7-16.1 h (median 7 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IT|IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo|in vitro; 4 agents, 4 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|other.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: other|plasma.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS/MS of methanol-deproteinised plasma; non-compartmental analysis in Phoenix WinNonlin.
- Body-quoted half-lives: fasudil in CAR-liposome (intravenous, sham rats): 0.7 ± 0.3 h [rat plasma - FASUDIL concentration, not peptide concentration] ;; fasudil in CAR-liposome (intravenous, PAH rats): 1.1 ± 0.3 h [rat plasma - FASUDIL concentration, not peptide concentration] ;; fasudil in CAR-liposome (intratracheal, sham rats): 12.9 ± 4.6 h [rat plasma - FASUDIL concentration, not peptide concentration] ;; fasudil in CAR-liposome (intratracheal, PAH rats): 16.1 ± 4.1 h [rat plasma - FASUDIL concentration, not peptide concentration].
- Caveats: THE MEASURED ANALYTE IS NOT THE PEPTIDE. All four PEPlife2 values verified against the 'fasudil in CAR-liposome' row of Table 4 (IV sham 0.7, IV PAH 1.1, IT sham 12.9, IT PAH 16.1 h), but every one is the plasma half-life of FASUDIL, a small-molecule Rho-kinase inhibitor. The CAR peptide (CARSKNKDC) is a heparan-sulphate-targeting ligand conjugated to the liposome surface; its own concentration is never measured. The paper attributes the extended half-life to liposome accumulation, slow drug release and absorption-controlled elimination, not to any property of the peptide - and the 18-fold spread between the IV and IT values for the identical peptide shows the label is set by the route and formulation, not the sequence. These four records (PEPlife2 IDs 5362-5365) have been excluded via CURATED_NOT_A_HALF_LIFE, because in a sequence-to-half-life training set they would assert that CARSKNKDC has a 16 h half-life.

Aneja R (2019) Pharmacokinetic stability of macrocyclic peptide triazole HIV-1 inactivators alone and in liposomes. *J Pept Sci*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1002/psc.3155

- Contributes **4 records**, 1 unique sequences; 4 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 4 of 4 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC6467816).
- Reported half-life: 0.029-8.87 h (median 1.48 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: N.A..
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo; 4 agents, 4 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: plasma|serum.
- Bioanalytical method: Plasma fluorescence (ex 494 nm, em 520 nm) against a FITC-AAR029b calibration curve, with UPLC-Q-TOF MS confirmation that the signal is intact FITC-AAR029b rather than free FITC or fragments.
- Body-quoted half-lives: FITC-AAR029b: 2.92 ± 0.93 h [rat plasma] ;; FITC-AAR029b in Liposome: 8.87 ± 3.17 h [rat plasma] ;; FITC-AAR029b (distribution phase): 0.029 ± 0.01 h [rat plasma] ;; FITC-AAR029b in Liposome (distribution phase): 0.032 ± 0.005 h [rat plasma].
- Caveats: All four PEPlife2 values match the body: elimination 2.92 ± 0.93 h (free) and 8.87 ± 3.17 h (liposomal), distribution 0.029 ± 0.01 and 0.032 ± 0.005 h. Two cautions. (1) n = 2 ANIMALS in total, two per group, run in duplicate - the SD of ±3.17 on the 8.87 h liposomal value spans more than a third of the mean, and no meaningful precision should be read into these numbers. (2) PEPlife2 leaves the Sequence field EMPTY on the two distribution-phase rows while filling it on the two elimination rows, although all four describe the same molecule; the empty rows will be dropped by any sequence-requiring filter, so this study contributes only its two elimination values downstream. Unlike a peptide-targeted carrier, the measured analyte here IS the peptide (fluorescence confirmed as intact FITC-AAR029b by Q-TOF), so the liposomal value is a legitimate peptide half-life. The body reports no dose in mg/kg, only 3 μg fluorescein equivalent.

SEQUENCE DEFECT: PEPlife2's string is HGEGTFTSDLSKQMEEEAVRLFIEWLKNGOPSSGAPPPS, which carries an 'O' where exenatide has a glycine (the native sequence is ...IEWLKNGGPSSGAPPPS). O is not one of the 20 standard residues; ESM-2 accepts it as a rare token but carries essentially no pretraining signal for it, so this row and its three siblings are embedded from a slightly corrupted sequence. The body names the drug only as 'Exenatide (mw. 4186.63)' and never prints a sequence, so the error cannot be repaired from the full text. The 24-fold gap between EXT-Sol and EXT-SBA-15 elimination half-lives is a property of the mesoporous silica carrier, not of the peptide: both rows carry the identical sequence, so this pair is a clean illustration of formulation dominating the label.

## PMID 29141139

Li D (2018) Effect of Synthetic High Density Lipoproteins Modification with Polyethylene Glycol on Pharmacokinetics and Pharmacodynamics. *Mol Pharm*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.molpharmaceut.7b00734

- Contributes **4 records**, 1 unique sequences; 4 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 4 of 4 records.
- Assay matrices: serum.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC6435036).
- Reported half-life: 4.25-7.14 h (median 4.82 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 4 agents, 4 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS (Agilent 6520 Q-TOF, negative ion) of methanol-precipitated serum with 5A peptide internal standard; 22A quantified as the sum of 22A and its 22A(-)Lys metabolite.
- Body-quoted half-lives: 22A peptide in sHDL (unmodified): 4.588 h (CV 8.58%) [rat serum, 22A peptide by LC-MS (22A plus its 22A(-)Lys metabolite)] ;; 22A peptide in sHDL-PEG2k (5%): 4.25 h (CV 17.4%) [rat serum, 22A peptide by LC-MS (22A plus its 22A(-)Lys metabolite)] ;; 22A peptide in sHDL-PEG2k (10%): 7.14 h (CV 28.8%) [rat serum, 22A peptide by LC-MS (22A plus its 22A(-)Lys metabolite)] ;; 22A peptide in sHDL-PEG5k (5%): 5.05 h (CV 6.34%) [rat serum, 22A peptide by LC-MS (22A plus its 22A(-)Lys metabolite)].
- Caveats: All four PEPlife2 values verified exactly against the T1/2 row of Table 2 (the PEPTIDE table): 4.588, 4.25, 7.14 and 5.05 h. Two cautions. (1) PEPlife2's Test Sample strings misstate the doses - it records 'after 2.5 mg/kg sHDL-PEG2k', 'after 5 mg/kg sHDL-PEG2k' and 'after 2.5 mg/kg sHDL-PEG5k', but the body states every group received 50 mg/kg and the 5/10 figures are PEG MOL PERCENT, not doses (sHDL-PEG2k 5%, sHDL-PEG2k 10%, sHDL-PEG5k 5%); PEPlife2 appears to have halved the percentages and relabelled them mg/kg. The half-life values themselves are correct. (2) Do not confuse these with Table 3, which gives the PHOSPHOLIPID half-lives (4.59, 8.62, 9.96, 12.5 h) for the same animals - the body's headline numbers '8.6 h for sHDL-PEG2k (5%) and 12.5 h for sHDL-PEG5k (5%)' are lipid, not peptide. The paper's own conclusion is that PEGylation did NOT change peptide elimination, so the apparent 4.25-to-7.14 h spread across these four rows is within-group variability (CV up to 28.8%, n = 4), not a PEG effect. The quantified analyte deliberately includes the 22A(-)Lys metabolite.

- Caveats: All three PEPlife2 values verified against the Results text: T20 1.22 h, T1144 7.48 h, FLT 27.09 h (the body rounds the last to 27.10). The body reports no dose, no strain, no sex and no group size for the rat PK study, so those fields are 'not reported'. The three agents differ enormously in size - T20 and T1144 are 36-37mers while FLT is a ~22 kDa monobody fusion of 163 residues - and the 22-fold half-life span tracks that, consistent with the albumin-binding-domain mechanism the paper describes. For context the paper notes T20's human half-life is 3.46-4.35 h and T1144's is 12-15 h, both substantially longer than the rat values here.

## PMID 26400022

Liu Z (2015) A novel stearic acid-modified hirudin peptidomimetic with improved pharmacokinetic properties and anticoagulant activity. *Sci Rep*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1038/srep14349

- Contributes **3 records**, 2 unique sequences; 3 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 3 of 3 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC4585835).
- Reported half-life: 0.217-3.54 h (median 0.25 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: N.A..
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 3 agents, 3 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Bioanalytical method: HPLC with MS-MS detection.
- Body-quoted half-lives: Hirulog-1 (bivalirudin): 15 ± 1.3 min [rat plasma] ;; Peptide 1 (hirudin mimetic): 13 ± 2.6 min [rat plasma] ;; Peptide S2 (stearic-acid-modified peptide 1): 212.2 ± 58.4 min [rat plasma].
- Caveats: All three PEPlife2 values are consistent with the body, which states peptide S2's T1/2 = 212.2 ± 58.4 min explicitly and gives 'T1/2 < 16 min' for hirulog-1 and peptide 1, bracketing PEPlife2's 15 ± 1.3 and 13 ± 2.6 min. IDENTICAL SEQUENCE, 16-FOLD DIFFERENT LABEL. PEPlife2 assigns peptide 1 and peptide S2 the SAME sequence string, fPRPGGGGQGDFEPIPEDAYDE, yet records 13 min for one and 212.2 min for the other. The body explains the difference entirely by a modification the string does not encode: S2 'in which the second Gly residue has been replaced with a modified Lys (stearic acid)'. A sequence-to-half-life model sees two contradictory labels for one input, and the 16-fold gap is attributable to a stearoyl group that is invisible in the representation. Note also that the lowercase f in both strings marks D-Phe; the training-set sequence cleaner upper-cases it, so the D-stereochemistry is lost and the residue is read as L-Phe.

| 2776 | Peptide S2 (Hirudin mimetic peptide) | 212.2 ±58.4 minutes | Sprague-Dawley (SD) rats plasma (Dose i/v injected) | in vivo | in vivo PK |

## PMID 36557850

Seong GS (2022) Determination of Leuprolide-Fatty Acid Conjugate in Rat Plasma Using LC-MS/MS and Its Pharmacokinetics after Subcutaneous Administration in Rats. *Molecules*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.3390/molecules27248716

- Contributes **3 records**, 2 unique sequences; 3 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 3 of 3 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC9786172).
- Reported half-life: 0.637-2.87 h (median 2.77 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: SC|SC (after administration of LOC).
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo; 3 agents, 3 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: sc.
- Bioanalytical method: Validated UPLC-MS/MS quantifying leuprolide and LOC simultaneously in rat plasma.
- Body-quoted half-lives: Leuprolide: 38.2 ± 4.3 min [rat plasma] ;; LOC (leuprolide-oleic acid conjugate): 172 ± 66 min [rat plasma] ;; Leuprolide released from LOC (metabolite): 166 ± 38 min [rat plasma (leuprolide measured after LOC dosing)].
- Caveats: Two of three PEPlife2 values verified directly in the body: leuprolide 38.2 ± 4.3 min and LOC 172 ± 66 min. The third, 166 ± 38 min, is Table 3's value for LEUPROLIDE MEASURED AS A METABOLITE after LOC dosing (Figure 4), and PEPlife2 labels its Test Sample accordingly. That third record needs care: it is not leuprolide's own half-life - the body measures that as 38.2 min - but the apparent half-life of leuprolide released from the conjugate, which is formation-rate-limited by LOC hydrolysis and therefore tracks LOC's 172 min. Filed against the leuprolide sequence it asserts a 4.3-fold longer half-life for the same molecule. SEQUENCE DEFECT: PEPlife2 gives LOC the string HWSYlLRP while giving leuprolide pGlu-HWSYlLRP. The conjugate is leuprolide PLUS oleic acid, so its string should extend the parent's, not drop the N-terminal pyroglutamate; the oleic acid is not encoded at all. As written the conjugate looks like a truncation of the parent while carrying a 4.5-fold longer half-life. The lowercase l marks D-Leu, which the training-set cleaner upper-cases, losing the stereochemistry.

| 4418 | Leuprolide | 166 ± 38 Minutes | Male SD rats plasma after LOC peptide administration | In Vivo | in vivo PK |

## PMID 36232550

Elfers CT (2022) Novel Long-Acting Oxytocin Analog with Increased Efficacy in Reducing Food Intake and Body Weight. *Int J Mol Sci*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.3390/ijms231911249

- Contributes **3 records** (1 distinct, the rest are exact repeats), 1 unique sequences; 3 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 3 of 3 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC9569447).
- Reported half-life: 2.3-2.3 h (median 2.3 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo; 1 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: sc.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS/MS per PEPlife2's Assay field.
- Body-quoted half-lives: ASK2131: 2.3 h [rat plasma].
- Caveats: The single PEPlife2 value of 2.3 h is verified exactly against the Results text, for subcutaneous dosing. Note tmax is 4 h against a 2.3 h half-life, so this profile is absorption-rate-limited (flip-flop) and the value reflects depot release rather than clearance. The measurement is also strongly affected by plasma binding: total Cmax is 276 nM while FREE Cmax is 0.6 nM, a 460-fold difference, so what decays with a 2.3 h half-life is almost entirely albumin-bound material. As with PF1 in 27217590, PEPlife2 stores the sequence CYIQNCGKG - the same string it uses for PF1 - while the lipid moiety that drives the pharmacokinetics is absent; two different compounds from two different papers therefore share one representation while carrying half-lives of 2.3 h and 8.5 h. For context the paper notes native oxytocin's half-life is ~5 min and the related analogue ASK1476's is 3.2 h. No dose or group size is given in the body.

- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 1 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: not_reported.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS/MS per PEPlife2's Assay field.

It is consistent with that description and is left uncorrected. Unlike the carrier-plus-payload records excluded elsewhere in this cohort, BT8009 is retained: the quantified species is the CONJUGATE itself, a 4.2 kDa bicyclic peptide covalently bearing MMAE, and the paper reports unconjugated MMAE separately and at low levels. So the label does describe the peptide entity. SEQUENCE ENCODING: the stored string carries four non-natural residue codes (1Nal, dC, HArg, HyP) and omits the sarcosine decamer spacer, the valine-citrulline cleavable linker and MMAE - together a large fraction of a 4.2 kDa molecule. No dose, route, strain or group size is given for the rat PK in the body.

## PMID 38092894

Chaung W (2023) Human cell-expressed tag-free rhMFG-E8 as an effective radiation mitigator. *Sci Rep*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-023-49499-y

- Contributes **3 records**, 1 unique sequences; 3 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 3 of 3 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC10719321).
- Reported half-life: 1.45-11.6 h (median 2.33 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 3 agents, 3 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Bioanalytical method: Human MFG-E8 Quantikine ELISA (R&D Systems MFGE80), which does not cross-react with rat MFG-E8; non-compartmental analysis in PKSolver.
- Body-quoted half-lives: tag-free rhMFG-E8 (beta phase, 2-6 h fit): 1.45 h [rat serum] ;; tag-free rhMFG-E8 (beta phase, 2-12 h fit): 2.33 h [rat serum] ;; tag-free rhMFG-E8 (alpha phase): 11.55 min [rat serum].
- Caveats: UNITS ERROR IN PEPlife2, now corrected. The body states the distribution half-life is 11.55 MINUTES; PEPlife2 recorded 11.55 with units 'Hours' (ID 4377), a 60-fold overstatement that also made the distribution phase eight times LONGER than the elimination phase it precedes - an impossibility the record's own numbers should have flagged. Corrected to minutes via UNITS_CORRECTIONS in subset_in_vivo_half_life.py, giving 0.1925 h. That record was in any case already excluded from the training set twice over: PEPlife2 leaves its Sequence field empty, and its phase is distribution rather than a concentration-decay terminal value. The two elimination values are NOT independent measurements: 1.45 h is the beta phase fitted over the 2-6 h window and 2.33 h is the SAME data refitted out to 12 h. The paper's own conclusion is hedged accordingly - 'the beta phase t1/2 of the tag-free rhMFG-E8 is calculated as at least 1.45 h'. Treating them as two observations double-counts one experiment and inflates the apparent spread. n is four independent experiments, not four animals.

- Contributes **3 records**, 1 unique sequences; 3 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.

- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC8359812).
- Reported half-life: 0.0139-2.6 h (median 0.467 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV|SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo; 3 agents, 3 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|sc.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: blood|other|plasma.
- Bioanalytical method: Radioimmunoassay (RIA), per PEPlife2's Assay field; the body does not describe the PK bioanalysis.

- Caveats: All three PEPlife2 values verified against the Introduction: K17F 50 s i.v., LIT01-196 28 min i.v. and 156 min s.c. ONE SEQUENCE, THREE LABELS SPANNING 187-FOLD. PEPlife2 assigns K17F and LIT01-196 the identical string KFRRQRPRLSHLGPMPF, so this study contributes three records with the same input and half-lives of 50 s, 28 min and 156 min. Two separate effects are invisible in that representation: LIT01-196 is K17F with a FLUOROCARBON CHAIN added to its N-terminus ('the original chemical addition of a fluorocarbon chain to the N-terminal part of K17F'), which accounts for the 34-fold jump from 50 s to 28 min; and the further 5.6-fold jump to 156 min is a ROUTE effect, i.v. versus s.c., which PEPlife2 also does not record. Note the half-lives are reported in the Introduction as prior results rather than measured in this paper, which is a blood-pressure pharmacology study; no dose, group size or bioanalytical method for the PK is given in the body.

## PMID 31341417

Pei D (2019) Anti-Tumor Activity and Pharmacokinetics of AP25-Fc Fusion Protein. *Int J Med Sci*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.7150/ijms.34365

- Contributes **2 records**, 2 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 2 of 2 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC6643120).
- Reported half-life: 0.833-56.3 h (median 28.6 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 2 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_reported.
- Bioanalytical method: Indirect competitive ELISA; parameters computed in DAS 1.0.
- Body-quoted half-lives: PSG4R (AP25-Fc fusion): 56.270 ± 15.398 h [rat plasma] ;; AP25: about 50 minutes [rat plasma].
- Caveats: Both PEPlife2 values verified against the body: PSG4R's elimination half-life is stated as 56.270 ± 15.398 h (PEPlife2 records 56.27), and AP25's as 'about 50 minutes'. The comparison is made explicitly - 'the elimination half-life of PSG4R (56 hours) was much longer than AP25 (around 50 minutes)'. Two caveats. The AP25 value is prior knowledge cited in the Introduction, not measured in this study, and it carries no dose, route, group size or dispersion; its approximate phrasing ('about', 'around') is lost once PEPlife2 stores it as the exact figure 50. And the PSG4R value has an SD of 15.4 h on a 56.3 h mean, with no group size given in the body, so its precision is weak. The 67-fold difference between the two is a real Fc-fusion effect and, unusually for this cohort, the two sequences DO differ - PSG4R carries a helical EAAAK linker and the Fc context that AP25 lacks - so this pair is informative rather than contradictory.

## PMID 31100806

Grishin AV (2019) The Influence of Dimerization on the Pharmacokinetics and Activity of an Antibacterial Enzyme Lysostaphin. *Molecules*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.3390/molecules24101879

- Contributes **2 records**, 2 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 2 of 2 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC6572387).
- Reported half-life: 1.5-3.1 h (median 2.3 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 2 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Bioanalytical method: Sandwich ELISA against lysostaphin antiserum raised in rabbits.
- Body-quoted half-lives: Lysostaphin: 1.5 ± 0.7 h [rat plasma] ;; Lst-HDD (helical dimerisation domain fusion): 3.1 ± 0.6 h [rat plasma].
- Caveats: Both PEPlife2 values verified exactly: lysostaphin 1.5 ± 0.7 h and Lst-HDD 3.1 ± 0.6 h (p = 0.01). IMPORTANT CONTEXT THAT THE RECORD LOSES: the paper states the elimination is biphasic and that ~95% of BOTH proteins had already been cleared by the first sampling point, 25 minutes after injection - only 2.8 and 1.6 μg/mL remained of a theoretical ~50 μg/mL. These terminal half-lives therefore describe the slow tail of a curve that has already lost almost the whole dose, and are not comparable with a single-phase half-life from a study sampled earlier. The AUC difference is only 2.6-fold, far less than the 10-fold the authors had seen for a covalently dimerised comparator. Note lysostaphin's PEPlife2 sequence carries a C-terminal His6 purification tag, which is part of the dosed material here but is an artefact of expression rather than a designed half-life feature. The body gives no strain, sex or group size.

## PMID 31480738

Wang C (2019) Long-Acting HIV-1 Fusion Inhibitory Peptides and their Mechanisms of Action. *Viruses*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.3390/v11090811

- Contributes **2 records**, 2 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 2 of 2 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC6784077).
- Reported half-life: 2.57-5.11 h (median 3.84 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo|in vitro; 2 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: sc.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: plasma.
- Bioanalytical method: INDIRECT: plasma concentration was estimated from an ex vivo anti-HIV-1 inhibition assay, taking the highest dilution causing 50% inhibition, rather than by a chemical assay.
- Body-quoted half-lives: PEG2kC34: 2.57 ± 0.71 h [rat plasma (activity-derived)] ;; PEG5kC34: 5.11 ± 3.54 h [rat plasma (activity-derived)].
- Caveats: Both PEPlife2 values verified exactly against the PK parameter table: PEG2kC34 2.57 ± 0.71 h and PEG5kC34 5.11 ± 3.54 h, both subcutaneous at 1.7 μmol/kg, n = 4 (2 male, 2 female). THE ASSAY IS A BIOACTIVITY PROXY, NOT A CONCENTRATION MEASUREMENT. Plasma levels were inferred from the dilution of plasma causing 50% inhibition of HIV-1 infection, so what decays with these half-lives is antiviral ACTIVITY; any inactive metabolite is invisible and any matrix effect on the assay reads as concentration. PEPlife2 records the assay as 'N.A.', losing that distinction. Note the SD on PEG5kC34 is 3.54 h on a 5.11 h mean, so the 2-fold separation between the two conjugates is not well resolved at n = 4. Finally, the two PEPlife2 sequences differ by a single leading cysteine (CWMEW... vs WMEW...), which is almost certainly the PEG attachment residue rather than a real design difference; as stored, the 2-fold half-life difference driven by 2 kDa vs 5 kDa of PEG is represented as the presence or absence of one Cys.

| 5336 | PEG5kC34 | 5.11 ± 3.54 Hours | Rats plasma | In Vivo | in vivo PK |

The per-agent figures come from the Figure 4B parameter panel. IDENTICAL SEQUENCE, 10-FOLD DIFFERENT LABEL: PEPlife2 assigns both agents the same enfuvirtide string, so the 2 kDa PEG that causes the entire effect is absent from the representation. Note also that both arms are subcutaneous with no intravenous comparator, and the body describes the conjugate as showing 'a slower but more sustained release profile from the injection site', so part of the difference is slower absorption rather than slower clearance.

## PMID 27217590

Modi ME (2016) Peripheral Administration of a Long-Acting Peptide Oxytocin Receptor Agonist Inhibits Fear-Induced Freezing. *J Pharmacol Exp Ther*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1124/jpet.116.232702

- Contributes **2 records**, 2 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.

- Reported half-life: 0.12-8.5 h (median 4.31 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo; 2 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS/MS per PEPlife2's Assay field; the body does not describe the bioanalysis.
- Body-quoted half-lives: OT (oxytocin): 0.12 h [rat plasma] ;; PF1 (PF-06655075): 8.5 h [rat plasma].
- Caveats: Both PEPlife2 values verified against the Results: oxytocin 0.12 h and PF1 8.5 h after intravenous dosing, a 71-fold difference. Unusually for this cohort the two sequences genuinely DO differ - PF1 is oxytocin with Pro7 to Gly and Leu8 to Lys - so the pair is not a contradictory input. However the modification that actually drives the half-life is not in the sequence: the body attributes it to a PALMITOYL group on that Lys8, 'a lipid tail that confers resistance to clearance' by binding albumin. The two-residue substitution visible in the string is described as a selectivity change (OTR over V1a), not a clearance change. The body reports no dose, strain, sex or group size for the rat PK.

Han J (2018) Rational design of dimeric lipidated Xenopus glucagon-like peptide 1 analogues as long-acting antihyperglycaemic agents. *Eur J Med Chem*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ejmech.2018.07.072

- Contributes **2 records**, 2 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 2 of 2 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction**.
- Reported half-life: 4.2-12.9 h (median 8.55 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo; 2 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: sc.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS/MS, per PEPlife2's Assay field; the accepted-manuscript PDF text does not describe the bioanalysis in a recoverable form.
- Body-quoted half-lives: Liraglutide: 4.2 ± 0.3 h (rat); 3.6 ± 0.2 h (mouse) [rat plasma] ;; Analogue 6: 12.9 ± 3.6 h (rat); 10.8 ± 0.8 h (mouse) [rat plasma].
- Caveats: Read from the publisher PDF (an accepted manuscript, so the text carries interleaved 'ACCEPTED MANUSCRIPT' watermark lines). Both PEPlife2 values verified exactly against the Results text: liraglutide 4.2 ± 0.3 h and analogue 6 12.9 ± 3.6 h in SD rats, both at 50 nmol/kg s.c. Two notes for downstream use. (1) Tmax is 2.0 h for liraglutide and 6.0 h for analogue 6, and both are subcutaneous, so part of the 3-fold half-life difference is slower absorption rather than slower clearance - the paper says so directly ('a slower absorption pattern of 6'). No intravenous arm is reported, so absorption and elimination cannot be separated. (2) PEPlife2's sequence for analogue 6 is a head-to-tail dimer of a 40-residue exendin-4-like sequence, consistent with the paper's dimerization strategy; liraglutide's string is the bare GLP-1 backbone with no palmitoyl protractor, so the lipidation that drives liraglutide's own half-life is absent from its representation.

The 8.22 h and 4.59 h values are that drug's circulation half-life in the carrier, and the paper's own comparison series (8.22 vs 4.59 vs 2.13 vs 0.47 h for the same drug in four different formulations) shows the label is set by the formulation, not by any peptide. The MTP peptide itself contains a D-Arg (lowercase r) and Dmt (2,6-dimethyltyrosine), neither of which survives one-letter cleaning. PEPlife2 IDs 4522 and 4523 dropped via CURATED_NOT_A_HALF_LIFE.

## PMID 35259149

Wijesinghe A (2022) Conjugates for use in peptide therapeutics: A systematic review and meta-analysis. *PLoS One*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0255753

- Contributes **2 records**, 1 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.

- Caveats: THIS IS A SYSTEMATIC REVIEW AND META-ANALYSIS, not a primary study - the title is 'Conjugates for use in peptide therapeutics: A systematic review and meta-analysis' and PubMed types it as a Review. It contributes 32 records to the cohort (30 mouse, 2 rat), the largest single review contribution, and every one of them is a value extracted from some other paper. The two rat records here, TP5-MA at 1.75 ± 0.72 h and TP5 at 0.022 ± 0.004 h, do not appear in the body text; the review's own narrative says only that unmodified peptides 'last minutes to a couple of hours'. Per the body-only rule both are recorded as not reported in body. They are NOT excluded from the dataset - they are plausibly accurate transcriptions of primary results - but they now carry the source_is_review flag added to build_study_dossier.py. Two further cautions: both records share the sequence RKDVY (thymopentin), so the 80-fold difference between them rests entirely on a modification the representation does not encode; and because a meta-analysis aggregates primary papers, these values may duplicate records the cohort already holds under their original PMIDs.

Liu Q (2021) Functional GLP-1R antibodies identified from a synthetic GPCR-focused library demonstrate potent blood glucose control. *MAbs*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1080/19420862.2021.1893425

- Contributes **2 records**, 2 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.

- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC7971233).

- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 2 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: not_reported.
- Bioanalytical method: ELISA per PEPlife2's Assay field; the body does not describe the bioanalysis.
- Body-quoted half-lives: TB01-3 (antagonist IgG): ~1 week [rat serum] ;; TB59-2 (agonist GLP-1 peptide-antibody fusion): >2 days [rat serum].
- Caveats: Both values verified against the Results and the Figure 11 caption: TB01-3 ~1 week and TB59-2 >2 days, n = 5 rats per group. Three caveats. (1) THE DOSED AGENTS ARE ANTIBODIES, not peptides - the body evaluates them 'in IgG format' - and the ~1-week half-life is explicitly attributed to antibody behaviour ('an antibody-like in vivo half-life', driven by FcRn recycling as the Introduction explains). PEPlife2 stores a 39-residue and a 30-residue motif against those labels, so the representation is a fragment of the molecule while the label belongs to the whole IgG. (2) Both values are approximate or bounded, not point estimates: '~1-week' and '>2-day'. PEPlife2 stores them as '~1' week and '>2' days, and the half-life parser takes the first number, so the open lower bound on TB59-2 becomes the fixed value 48 h. (3) The body reports no dose and no route for the rat PK study. For scale the paper notes liraglutide's half-life is 13 h.

## PMID 33374407

Binder U (2020) PASylated Thymosin α1: A Long-Acting Immunostimulatory Peptide for Applications in Oncology and Virology. *Int J Mol Sci*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.3390/ijms22010124

- Contributes **2 records**, 1 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 2 of 2 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC7795856).
- Reported half-life: 15.7-15.9 h (median 15.8 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo; 2 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: sc.
- Bioanalytical method: Quantitative sandwich ELISA developed to detect only Tα1-PAS and not endogenous rat Tα1.
- Body-quoted half-lives: Tα1-PAS (alpha phase): 15.7 ± 0.8 h [rat plasma] ;; Tα1-PAS (beta phase): 15.9 ± 0.9 h [rat plasma].
- Caveats: Both PEPlife2 values verified exactly against Table 1: tau1/2 alpha 15.7 ± 0.8 h and tau1/2 beta 15.9 ± 0.9 h. The two phases are within 0.2 h of each other, so these are effectively one number reported twice rather than two separable phases, and counting both double-weights this single N = 5 experiment. The body itself quotes only the beta value. REPRESENTATION MISMATCH: PEPlife2's sequence is the bare 28-residue thymosin alpha 1 peptide, but the dosed agent is Tα1 fused to a 600-RESIDUE PAS polypeptide - the paper's whole point, 'fusion with a 600-residue polypeptide comprising the small natural L-amino acids Pro, Ala, and Ser', biophysically mimicking PEG. The stored sequence is therefore about 5% of the molecule by length, while the label is entirely a product of the missing 95%. For reference the body gives the native peptide's published rat half-life as 1.9 h. Note also tmax is 22.7 h, longer than the half-life itself, so this subcutaneous profile is absorption-rate-limited (flip-flop) and the terminal value reflects depot release rather than clearance.

METHODOLOGICAL CAVEAT: the half-life was not fitted - it is read directly off Figure 3 as the time at which concentration fell to half its initial value ('The peptide reached to the half of its initial concentration ... at 90 min making the plasma half-life of the peptide from the plot is 90 min'). That is a graphical time-to-50% rather than an exponential elimination half-life, and the two coincide only for a single-compartment first-order decay sampled from t=0. No dose, group size, dispersion, strain detail or fitting method is given in the body. The concentrations involved are very low (half of the initial value is 1.16 ng/mL), so the measurement sits near the assay's likely limit of quantitation.

Tanner MR (2017) Prolonged immunomodulation in inflammatory arthritis using the selective Kv1.3 channel blocker HsTX1[R14A] and its PEGylated analog. *Clin Immunol*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.clim.2017.03.014

- Contributes **1 records**, 1 unique sequences; 1 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.

- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC5484050).
- Reported half-life: 37.3-37.3 h (median 37.3 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 1 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: sc.
- Bioanalytical method: Anti-PEG ELISA (PEGylated protein ELISA kit, Enzo Life Sciences); single exponential decay fit.
- Body-quoted half-lives: PEG-HsTX1[R14A]: 37.3 h [rat serum - PEG detected, not the peptide].
- Caveats: The single PEPlife2 value of 37.3 h is verified against the Results text. TWO CAVEATS. (1) THE ASSAY DETECTS PEG, NOT THE PEPTIDE - quantitation is by an anti-PEG antibody ELISA, so the decay measured is that of the PEG moiety and would not distinguish intact conjugate from PEG still carrying a degraded or cleaved peptide. PEPlife2 records the assay as 'Anti-PEG ELISA', which preserves this, but the half-life is filed against the peptide sequence. (2) tmax is 24 h against a 37.3 h half-life, so this subcutaneous profile is substantially absorption-limited and the terminal value is not a pure clearance half-life. The PEG that causes the effect is not in the stored sequence; the body notes comparable unPEGylated peptide Kv1.3 blockers have rat half-lives 'of less than one hour', so essentially the entire 37-fold extension is attributable to the unencoded moiety. No group size or dispersion on the half-life is given.

## PMID 30165332

Zhu Y (2018) LC-MS/MS assay for the determination of a novel D-peptide antagonist of CXCR4 in rat plasma and its application to a preclinical pharmacokinetic study. *J Pharm Biomed Anal*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jpba.2018.08.032

- Contributes **1 records**, 1 unique sequences; 1 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 1 of 1 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC10264084).
- Reported half-life: 8.7-8.7 h (median 8.7 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo; 1 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: sc.
- Bioanalytical method: HPLC-MS/MS, validated over 10-10000 ng/mL (r > 0.998), with organic additives added to suppress peptide adsorption.
- Body-quoted half-lives: DV1: 8.7 ± 2.4 h [rat plasma].
- Caveats: PEPlife2's value of 8.7 ± 2.4 h could NOT be verified numerically: the derived parameters are in a table INSET INTO FIGURE 5, which the PMC BioC deposit does not carry as text. The body confirms the qualitative claim - 'The long half time (t1/2) and the slow reduction after 12 h were observed' - and independently gives Tmax 0.83 ± 0.29 h and Cmax 8592 ± 1056 ng/mL after 10 mg/kg subcutaneous, which PEPlife2 does not record. The value is left uncorrected. STEREOCHEMISTRY LOST: PEPlife2's sequence is written entirely in lowercase, the convention marking an all-D peptide, and the body confirms DV1 is 'a novel D-peptide antagonist of CXCR4'. The training-set sequence cleaner upper-cases it, so an all-D peptide and its all-L enantiomer become the same input - while protease resistance, and therefore much of the half-life, is exactly what the D-configuration buys. The body also attributes the slow terminal phase to tissue redistribution rather than to slow clearance.

| ID | peptide | half-life | matrix (`Test Sample`) | flag | design |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5657 | DV1 | 8.7 ± 2.4 Hours | Rats plasma  | In Vivo | in vivo PK |

## PMID 29721065

Feng J (2018) Development of a novel albumin-based and maleimidopropionic acid-conjugated peptide with prolonged half-life and increased in vivo anti-tumor efficacy. *Theranostics*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.7150/thno.22069

- Contributes **1 records**, 1 unique sequences; 1 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 1 of 1 records.
- Assay matrices: blood.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC5928873).
- Reported half-life: 6.97-6.97 h (median 6.97 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo; 1 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Bioanalytical method: Gamma counting of 64Cu radioactivity in whole blood; two-compartment fit in GraphPad Prism 5.01.
- Body-quoted half-lives: 64Cu-DOTA-F56-CM: 6.967 h [rat blood - total 64Cu radioactivity by gamma counting].
- Caveats: The single PEPlife2 value of 6.967 h is consistent with the described two-compartment analysis of gamma-counted blood radioactivity; the figure itself is not restated in the body text so it could not be checked digit for digit. Caveats. (1) n = 2 RATS per group, so no meaningful precision, and no dispersion is reported. (2) The readout is TOTAL 64Cu RADIOACTIVITY, not intact conjugate - if 64Cu dissociates from the DOTA chelator the label keeps being counted, which for copper-DOTA over a 96 h window is a real possibility. Unlike the total-radioactivity case in 36588717 the paper does not report a parent-drug comparator, so the extent of the discrepancy is unknown. (3) The stored sequence is the bare F56 peptide; the DOTA chelator, the copper and the albumin-binding CM moiety - which the introduction identifies as the half-life-extension mechanism - are all absent from it. The paper notes rat albumin's own half-life is 1.9 days.

PEPlife2's value of 1.21 h for peptide 11 could NOT be verified: it sits in Table 5, whose 'rat T1/2 (h)' column the PDF text extraction split across lines so that values cannot be matched to rows with confidence. Per the protocol's PDF caution the half-life is recorded as not reported rather than guessed, and PEPlife2's figure is left uncorrected. The study design IS recoverable and confirms the record's plausibility: a 1 h intravenous infusion into non-fasted male Sprague Dawley rats, n = 3, femoral vein cannula, sampling 0.25-48 h, UPLC-MS/MS. Note the dosing is an INFUSION rather than a bolus, which PEPlife2 does not record, and the paper's design point is that the fatty acid moiety and staple position drive the half-life - neither is present in the stored sequence.

The stored sequence also retains the FLAG purification tags, which are an expression artefact. No strain, sex or group size is given for the rat arm; Table 1's n = 3 applies to the mouse conjugate panel.

- Body-quoted half-lives: CP (comb polymer): 14.6 ± 2.9 h [rat plasma - total radioactivity by liquid scintillation counting].
- Caveats: The PEPlife2 value of 14.6 ± 2.9 h matches the body's '14.6 h' for CP, described as intermediate between the linear polymer's 2.7 h and the bottlebrush polymers' >35 h. THE SEQUENCE IS NOT A PEPTIDE SEQUENCE. PEPlife2 stores 'lKlW' - four characters in mixed case, marking alternating D- and L-residues - which is a monomer or repeat unit of a polymer architecture, not a molecule. The label is set by that architecture (linear vs comb vs bottlebrush spans 2.7 to >35 h for related materials), none of which the string encodes; after one-letter cleaning it becomes the four residues LKLW and the D-configuration is lost as well. The readout is also total radioactivity by scintillation counting rather than intact-species concentration, and the body gives no strain, sex, group size, dose or route.

| ID | peptide | half-life | matrix (`Test Sample`) | flag | design |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4614 | CP | 14.6 ± 2.9 (Elimination Half Life) Hours | Rats plasma | In Vivo | in vivo PK |

PEPlife2 files the 101.3 min value against the sequence GGGGDRVYIHPF - a GGGG linker plus angiotensin II - whose own concentration is never measured. - Caveats: EXCLUDED FROM THE TRAINING SET. The measured analyte is TIGECYCLINE, a tetracycline antibiotic - the paper introduces it as 'Tigecycline (TGC), a new-generation tetracycline antibiotic' - loaded into Tween-80-coated chitosan nanoparticles that display an amyloid-beta fragment as a brain-targeting ligand. The paper's own three-arm comparison settles the attribution: the SAME drug has a half-life of 0.77 h free, 0.91 h in plain chitosan nanoparticles and 1.26 h in the targeted ones. PEPlife2 files the 1.26 h value against the sequence CGSNKGAIIGLM, whose own concentration is never measured. PEPlife2 ID 4037 dropped via CURATED_NOT_A_HALF_LIFE.

SEQUENCE ENCODING: the stored string contains three Aib residues and one Nle as hyphen-delimited three-letter codes, which previously expanded into nine spurious standard residues under one-letter cleaning; clean_sequence now collapses each to a single X.
